pointwise domination is sufficient but is not necessary.

\begin{theorem}[One-sided favorable-model transfer]
\label{thm:one-sided-prime-model-transfer}
Fix the finite character portfolio and its capacities $A(p)$,
and let $1/2\le\eta<1$. The following are equivalent.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The reference $\mathcal Z_{\mathcal Q}$ is zero-free
on $\Re s>\eta$.
\item There exist real weights $w_p\ge0$, with
$\sum_p w_p p^{-\sigma}<\infty$ for every $\sigma>1$,
whose initial full Euler product
\[
                  F_w(s)=\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-w_p}
\]
has a holomorphic nowhere-zero continuation on
$\{\Re s>\eta\}\setminus\{1\}$, a pole of order $R$ at $1$,
and satisfies the one-sided size bound
\begin{equation}
 \forall\epsilon>0\qquad
 E(X):=\sum_{p\le X}(w_p-A(p))_+
             \ll_\epsilon X^{\eta+\epsilon}\quad(X\ge2).
 \label{eq:one-sided-overweight-size}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
For $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$ and $\eta=1/2$, this is an RH
equivalence. For a general portfolio at $\eta=1/2$ it is
the zero-free assertion for all of its Dirichlet
$L$-functions, including $\zeta$.

More generally, if a particular $F_w$ has the stated
favorable continuation on $\Re s>\tau$ with $1/2\le\tau<1$,
put
\[
 E_j=\sum_{2^j\le p<2^{j+1}}(w_p-A(p))_+ .
\]
Any reference zero of real part $\beta>\tau$ forces
\begin{equation}
 \limsup_{j\to\infty}\frac{\log(1+E_j)}{j\log2}\ge\beta.
 \label{eq:overweight-zero-readout}
\end{equation}
Thus a favorable prime model cannot erase a hypothetical
bad-zero response while keeping its positive overweight
smaller than that response.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Set $e_p=(w_p-A(p))_+$.
Dyadic summation shows that
\eqref{eq:one-sided-overweight-size} is equivalent to
$\sum_p e_p p^{-\sigma}<\infty$ for every $\sigma>\eta$.
For the converse direction of this elementary equivalence,
use $E(X)\le X^\sigma\sum_p e_p p^{-\sigma}$.
Accordingly
\[
                  E_+(s)=\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-e_p}
\]
is an absolutely convergent holomorphic unit on $\Re s>\eta$,
with every prime power retained.

Under (2), the complete quotient is initially
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\mathcal Z_{\mathcal Q}(s)E_+(s)}{F_w(s)}
   =\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-(A(p)-w_p)_+}\,U_{\mathcal Q}(s).
 \label{eq:overweight-positive-quotient}
\end{equation}
Its coefficients are nonnegative real numbers.
Its continuation is holomorphic on $\Re s>\eta$:
$F_w$ is zero-free and its pole of order $R$ cancels the
reference pole, while $E_+$ is a unit.
The positive Dirichlet continuation principle therefore
gives actual series convergence throughout this half-plane.
The finite-positive-product and logarithm argument in
Theorem~\ref{thm:reverse-prime-capacity} makes the full
quotient a unit there. Equation
\eqref{eq:overweight-positive-quotient} then proves that
$\mathcal Z_{\mathcal Q}$ is zero-free. This proves (2)
implies (1).

Conversely, under (1) choose $w_p=A(p)$.
Then $F_w=\mathcal Z_{\mathcal Q}/U_{\mathcal Q}$ has the
required favorable continuation and pole order, and $E(X)=0$.
For $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$ the reference is $\zeta$; the usual
functional-equation symmetry identifies its zero-free
open right half of the critical strip with RH.
For other portfolios, the reference's factors have no poles
away from $1$, so its zero-free assertion is exactly the
corresponding assertion for every included factor.

For the last statement, if the displayed limsup were smaller
than $\beta>\tau$, choose an exponent strictly between their
maximum and $\beta$. Dyadic summation makes $E_+$ a unit
above that exponent, and the same quotient proof excludes
the zero, a contradiction.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The necessary size of the selected set.}
Let $Q_w=\{p:w_p>0\}$ and $A_*=\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}\varphi(q)>0$.
Write $W_j=\sum_{2^j\le p<2^{j+1}}w_p$. Since $A(p)\le A_*$,
the exact one-sided comparison gives
\begin{equation}
 \#(Q_w\cap[2^j,2^{j+1}))
       \ge\frac{(W_j-E_j)_+}{A_*}.
 \label{eq:overweight-support-size}
\end{equation}
Indeed $w_p\le A(p)+e_p$ on each selected prime, and both
$w_p$ and $e_p$ vanish outside the selected set.
For a prime quadrature with
$W_j=R\nu([u,2u))$, $u=2^j$, the explicit density lower bound
above gives $W_j\ge R\ell_u u$.
If $E_j\ll_\epsilon u^{\eta+\epsilon}$ with $\eta<1$,
this forces selected support of order at least $u/\log u$,
with constants depending only on the fixed portfolio.
A small support with large weights must pay for those weights
in $E_j$; its cardinality alone is not the signal-retention test.

The converse in the equivalence uses the zero-free assertion
itself. The next theorem supplies an independently favorable
model on actual primes, completing the construction part of
the proposed reverse order. Its one-sided overweight still
requires a separate arithmetic estimate. The transfer theorem
identifies the exact sufficient size condition and its
noncancelling zero response. Its proof is ordinary, not yet
a separate Lean declaration, and the certified unconditional
reference boundary remains $7/8$.


\subsection{An unconditional favorable model on actual primes}
\label{sec:actual-favorable-prime-model}

The favorable model can be built on actual primes before estimating
the size of its overweight. Only a weak classical prime-gap input
is needed to guarantee that the finite moment problems have solutions:
there are constants $C_g>0$ and $p_0$ such that consecutive primes
$p<p'$ with $p\ge p_0$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
                         p'-p\le C_g p^{2/3}.
 \label{eq:weak-prime-grid-input}
\end{equation}
This follows, for example, from the stronger short-interval
theorem of Baker--Harman--Pintz~\cite[Theorem~1]{BakerHarmanPintz}.
It is used only for the availability of prime locations.
It supplies no estimate for the one-sided overweight below.

\begin{theorem}[Actual prime model with optimal local overweight]
\label{thm:actual-favorable-prime-model}
Fix the finite character portfolio. There is a fixed integer $J_0$
such that the following construction works for every $j\ge J_0$.
Partition $[u,2u)$, $u=2^j$, into half-open cells of length
$h=2^{\lfloor3j/4\rfloor}$. For a cell $I=[a,b)$ put
\[
 H_I=R\nu(I),\qquad M_I=R\int_I x\,d\nu(x),\qquad m_I=M_I/H_I,
\]
and let $S_I$ be its actual primes, with extreme primes $p_-,p_+$.
They satisfy $p_-<m_I<p_+$.
Choose a nonnegative weight vector solving the finite problem
\begin{equation}
 \min\ \sum_{p\in S_I}(w_p-A(p))_+,\qquad
 \sum_{p\in S_I}w_p=H_I,\qquad
 \sum_{p\in S_I}p\,w_p=M_I .
 \label{eq:actual-prime-overweight-program}
\end{equation}
A minimizer exists. Fix it, for example, by lexicographic
minimization of the ordered coordinates among all minimizers.
Set $w_p=0$ for primes below $2^{J_0}$.

The resulting actual prime Euler product has an unconditional
continuation
\begin{equation}
 F_w(s)=\left(\frac{s}{s-1}\right)^R e^{G(s)}
                                  \qquad(\Re s>1/2),
 \label{eq:actual-favorable-prime-product}
\end{equation}
where $G$ is holomorphic. Thus $F_w$ is zero-free on this
half-plane and has precisely a pole of order $R$ at $1$.

Let $E_I$ denote the optimal value in
\eqref{eq:actual-prime-overweight-program}, and let $\delta_I^*$
be the ideal two-moment defect from
Theorem~\ref{thm:two-moment-finite-capacity} on the original
cell hull $[a,b]$, with the original mass $H_I$, moment $M_I$
and actual prime capacities. Then
\begin{equation}
 \delta_I^*\le E_I\le K_I\delta_I^*,\qquad
 K_I=\max\left\{1,\frac{m_I-a}{m_I-p_-},
                         \frac{b-m_I}{p_+-m_I}\right\}.
 \label{eq:actual-ideal-defect-comparison}
\end{equation}
All these denominators are positive. Uniformly over the cells,
\begin{equation}
 1\le K_I\le1+C_1u^{-1/12},\qquad
 C_1=\frac{4C_g\,2^{2/3}}{c_0},\quad c_0=\frac1{8\zeta(2)}.
 \label{eq:actual-prime-mesh-loss}
\end{equation}
In particular passing from the ideal defect to actual prime
weights loses neither a power nor an asymptotic leading coefficient.
The model is independently favorable; excluding reference zeros
still requires the separate overweight estimate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On $[u,2u]$ the model density lies between $\ell_u$ and
$r_u=1/\log u$. Therefore the two distances of its cell mean
from the endpoints obey
\[
 m_I-a,\ b-m_I\ge\frac{\ell_u}{2r_u}h\ge c_0h.
\]
The last inequality uses $u\ge2$,
$1-u^{-1}\ge1/2$, and $\log u/\log(2u)\ge1/2$.

For all sufficiently large $u$, the prime-gap bound gives
\[
 0\le p_--a\le g_u,\qquad
 0<b-p_+\le g_u,\qquad g_u=C_g(2u)^{2/3}.
\]
For the left edge use the prime just preceding $a$, unless
$a$ itself is prime; for the right edge use the prime preceding
$b$, since $b$ is excluded. Bertrand's theorem ensures that
these preceding primes are at least $u/2$ when $u$ is large,
so the starting threshold $p_0$ is respected.
Because $h\ge u^{3/4}/2$, choose $J_0\ge2$ with
\[
 2^{J_0}>2p_0+2,\qquad
 2C_g2^{2/3}2^{-J_0/12}\le c_0/2.
\]
These monotone conditions hold at every later block and give
$g_u\le c_0h/2$. In particular there are actual primes on both
sides of $m_I$. No numerical value for the classical starting
threshold is asserted.

Nonnegative weights on just $p_-,p_+$ can already match
$H_I,M_I$, by the barycentric formulas
\[
 w_-=\frac{H_I(p_+-m_I)}{p_+-p_-},\qquad
 w_+=\frac{H_I(m_I-p_-)}{p_+-p_-}.
\]
The feasible set of all weight vectors is nonempty and compact:
it is closed and every coordinate lies in $[0,H_I]$.
The overweight objective is continuous, so it attains its
minimum. Successive coordinate minima on its compact minimizer
set justify the stated tie rule.

To identify the minimum, consider the capped moment-defect
problem on the smaller hull $[p_-,p_+]$, with model measure
$H_I\delta_{m_I}$. This is only a device for this finite
moment problem: its mass and first moment are exactly the
original $H_I,M_I$. The continuous model used in the analytic
comparison remains $R\nu|_I$.
Let its optimal defect be $\delta'_I$.
Any feasible actual $w$ can be clipped to
$v_p=\min(w_p,A(p))$; its removed weights give a positive
actual-prime defect of mass $\sum_p(w_p-A(p))_+$.
Thus $\delta'_I\le E_I$.
Conversely, redistribute any optimal positive defect on
$[p_-,p_+]$ to the two actual extreme primes by barycentric
weights, preserving its mass and first moment. Add those
weights to the capped vector. The resulting actual vector
is feasible for \eqref{eq:actual-prime-overweight-program},
and its positive overweight is at most the added mass.
Hence $E_I\le\delta'_I$, proving equality.

The finite strike formula for $\delta'_I$ has exactly the
same numerators as the original ideal formula. Its left
strikes are the actual primes above $m_I$, including $p_+$,
and its right strikes are the actual primes below $m_I$,
including $p_-$. Its denominators are respectively $c-p_-$
and $p_+-c$. The ideal formula tests these same locations
with denominators $c-a$ and $b-c$.
For $c>m_I$,
\[
 \frac{c-a}{c-p_-}\le\frac{m_I-a}{m_I-p_-};
\]
the symmetric right ratio is bounded by
$(b-m_I)/(p_+-m_I)$.
If a numerator is negative it is already dominated by the
zero test; otherwise these ratios bound the corresponding
objectives. The common tests $0,H_I-C_I$ require no factor.
This proves $E_I\le K_I\delta_I^*$.
Every smaller-hull defect is feasible on the larger cell
hull, so $\delta_I^*\le\delta'_I=E_I$.

Finally
\[
 K_I\le1+\frac{g_u}{c_0h-g_u}
      \le1+\frac{2g_u}{c_0h}
      \le1+C_1u^{-1/12},
\]
which proves the asserted uniform loss.

It remains to verify favorability independently of the
overweight size. Every block has exact total weight
$R\nu([u,2u))\ll_Ru$, so the full prime product converges
absolutely for $\Re s>1$.
The complete signed logarithmic identity there is
\begin{gather*}
 \log F_w(s)-R\log M(s)
 =-R\int_1^{2^{J_0}}f_s(x)\,d\nu(x)+\sum_{j\ge J_0}G_j(s),\\
 G_j(s)=\sum_{I\subset[2^j,2^{j+1})}
       \left[\sum_{p\in S_I}w_pf_s(p)
                          -R\int_I f_s(x)\,d\nu(x)\right].
\end{gather*}
Both the constant and linear Taylor terms cancel in every
cell. The $k=2$ remainder calculation already proved above
gives the full block bound
\begin{equation}
 |G_j(s)|\le
 \frac{R}{\log2}|s|(|s|+1)
              \frac{u^{1/2-\Re s}}{(1-u^{-\Re s})^2}.
 \label{eq:actual-prime-model-error}
\end{equation}
It converges normally on $\Re s>1/2$.
The retained finite initial integral is holomorphic on
$\Re s>0$, including its integrable endpoint at $1$.
Consequently the right side defines a holomorphic $G$ on
$\Re s>1/2$, and exponentiation proves
\eqref{eq:actual-favorable-prime-product}.
This proves the favorable continuation without assuming
any zero-free assertion for the reference.
\end{proof}

For this explicit optimized model, put
$E_j=\sum_{I\subset[u,2u)}E_I$ and
$\Delta_j^*=\sum_{I\subset[u,2u)}\delta_I^*$.
The complete comparison is
\begin{equation}
 \Delta_j^*\le E_j\le(1+C_1u^{-1/12})\Delta_j^*.
 \label{eq:actual-prime-complete-consumer}
\end{equation}
An independent estimate $E_j\ll_\epsilon u^{3/4+\epsilon}$
therefore gives boundary $3/4$ by
Theorem~\ref{thm:one-sided-prime-model-transfer};
exponent $1/2$ would imply RH and the corresponding
zero-free assertions for the included characters.
Every hypothetical reference zero with real part $\beta>1/2$
forces the dyadic overweight exponent to be at least $\beta$.
The ideal and actual exponents agree by
\eqref{eq:actual-prime-complete-consumer}.
This completes the construction and signal-retention parts
of the reverse strategy.

The remaining arithmetic problem is the full finite-strike
sum from \eqref{eq:two-moment-full-signed-consumer}, including
its total mass deficit and both boundary tests.
Unconditionally $E_I\le H_I$, so
$E_j\le R\nu([u,2u))\ll_Ru/\log u$.
That logarithmic bound supplies no new fixed power saving.
The favorable model is not $\zeta$; its positive overweight
still carries the potential zeros. The certified reference
boundary remains $7/8$.
This actual-prime construction and comparison have ordinary
proofs here and consume the cited classical prime-gap theorem;
they have not yet been formalized in Lean.
No literature priority is asserted.

\subsubsection{Higher moments and sparse repair primes}
\label{sec:higher-moment-repair-primes}

The actual construction extends to every fixed moment order.
Its overweight can moreover be confined to a sparse set of actual
primes. This is sparsity of the repair, not an estimate for its
weighted mass.

\begin{lemma}[Positive-objective polynomial norming]
\label{lem:positive-objective-prime-norming}
Let $I=[a,b]$, $h=b-a>0$, and let $\mu$ have density between
$\ell$ and $r$, where $0<\ell\le r$. Let a finite set $S\subset I$
have covering radius at most $\epsilon h$ on $I$. For an integer
$d\ge1$ put
\[
 B_d=\frac{2^d d^{d+1}}{d!},\qquad C_d=4B_d^2r/\ell.
\]
If $C_d\epsilon<1$, every real polynomial $P$ of degree at most
$d$ with $\int_I P\,d\mu>0$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
 0<\max_I P\le
       \frac{\max_{x\in S}P(x)}{1-C_d\epsilon}.
 \label{eq:positive-objective-norming}
\end{equation}
In particular the moment vector of $\mu/\mu(I)$ through degree
$d$ belongs to the convex hull of the corresponding moment
vectors of the actual nodes.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $L=\max_I|P|$. Interpolation at the $d+1$ nodes
$a+ih/d$, $0\le i\le d$, gives the elementary derivative bound
\[
 \max_I|P'|\le \frac{B_d}{h}L.
\]
Indeed, the denominator of the $i$th Lagrange basis polynomial
has absolute value $(h/d)^d i!(d-i)!$. Its derivative numerator
is a sum of $d$ products, each bounded by $h^{d-1}$ on $I$.
Summing these bounds and using
$\sum_{i=0}^d1/(i!(d-i)!)=2^d/d!$ proves the assertion.
This argument also covers constant polynomials represented
on this interpolation grid.

Let $M=\max_I P>0$. If $L=M$, then $L\le4B_d(r/\ell)M$.
Otherwise $P$ attains $-L$. On the intersection of $I$ with a
radius $h/(2B_d)$ interval about such a point it is at most
$-L/2$. This intersection has length at least $h/(2B_d)$;
here $B_d\ge2$. Consequently
\[
 \int_I P_-\,d\mu\ge\frac{\ell hL}{4B_d},
 \qquad
 \int_I P_+\,d\mu\le rhM.
\]
The positive integral gives $L\le4B_d(r/\ell)M$ in this case
as well. A node within $\epsilon h$ of a positive maximum
therefore has value at least
$M-B_d\epsilon L\ge(1-C_d\epsilon)M$.
This proves \eqref{eq:positive-objective-norming} with all
signs retained.

If the normalized moment vector were outside the finite
convex hull, strict separation would give a polynomial whose
$\mu$-average is larger than its maximum on $S$. Subtract
that node maximum. The resulting polynomial has positive
integral and nonpositive values at every node, contradicting
\eqref{eq:positive-objective-norming}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Actual overweight dual and sparse attainment]
\label{lem:actual-overweight-dual-sparse}
Let $k\ge2$, let $\mu$ be a positive measure of mass $H>0$
on a compact interval $I$, and let $S\subset I$ be finite.
Give its nodes capacities $A_x\ge0$. Suppose nonnegative
weights on $S$ can match the moments of $\mu$ through degree
$k-1$. Let $E$ be the minimum positive overweight among
such weights, and let $\delta^*$ be the ideal capped defect
on the whole interval from
Theorem~\ref{thm:capped-positive-moment-selection}. Then
\begin{equation}
 E=\sup_{\substack{\deg P<k\\P(x)\le1\ (x\in S)}}
       \left(\int_I P\,d\mu-\sum_{x\in S}A_x(P(x))_+\right).
 \label{eq:actual-overweight-polynomial-dual}
\end{equation}
A minimizer exists with $(w_x-A_x)_+>0$ on at most $k$
nodes. If the norming conclusion
\eqref{eq:positive-objective-norming} holds with
$C_d\epsilon=t<1$, $d=k-1$, then
\begin{equation}
 \delta^*\le E\le(1-t)^{-1}\delta^*.
 \label{eq:all-moment-actual-ideal-comparison}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The feasible weights form a nonempty compact set: their
zeroth moment is $H$, so each coordinate is in $[0,H]$.
The continuous overweight objective therefore attains its
minimum.

Put $z(x)=(1,x,\ldots,x^{k-1})$ and $c=\int_I z\,d\mu$.
For $\delta\ge0$ the capped weights with actual-node repair
mass at most $\delta$ have the compact convex moment set
\[
 K_\delta=
 \left\{\sum_{x\in S}v_xz(x):0\le v_x\le A_x\right\}
 +\delta\operatorname{conv}\bigl(\{0\}\cup\{z(x):x\in S\}\bigr).
\]
Clipping $w$ to $\min(w,A)$ shows $c\in K_E$.
Conversely, $c\in K_\delta$ gives feasible actual weights
with overweight at most $\delta$. Hence $c\notin K_\delta$
when $0\le\delta<E$.

Strict separation in that case gives a polynomial $P$ with
\[
 \int_I P\,d\mu>
       \sum_{x\in S}A_x(P(x))_+
             +\delta\max\{0,\max_S P\}.
\]
Its node maximum is positive: otherwise any already feasible
nonnegative actual quadrature would make its integral
nonpositive, contrary to this strict inequality.
Normalize by that maximum. The resulting dual objective is
larger than $\delta$. Weak duality follows directly, node by
node, from $P(x)\le1$:
\[
 w_xP(x)-A_x(P(x))_+\le(w_x-A_x)_+.
\]
Letting $\delta$ increase to $E$ proves the dual equality;
if $E=0$, the zero polynomial supplies equality.

For sparse attainment start with an optimal $w$ and write
$v=\min(w,A)$, $e=(w-A)_+$. A nonnegative combination
$\sum e_xz(x)$ can be reduced to at most $k$ nodes while
preserving that entire vector. If more than $k$ vectors
remain, take a linear dependence among them. Its coefficients
sum to zero by the first coordinate, so they have both signs.
Move the positive weights along that dependence until one
vanishes, and repeat. Denote the resulting weights by $e'$.
They have the same mass $E$. Then $w'=v+e'$ matches all
moments and has overweight at most $E$, hence exactly $E$
by optimality. Its positive overweight is supported among
the at most $k$ nodes supporting $e'$.

Clipping also makes every actual minimizer an admissible
ideal capped-defect solution, proving $\delta^*\le E$.
For the other inequality take a polynomial in the actual
dual with positive objective. Its integral is positive, so
norming gives $M=\max_I P\le(1-t)^{-1}$.
The polynomial $P/M$ is at most $1$ on all of $I$.
The exact ideal dual already proved above, and positive
homogeneity of its objective, give an actual objective at
most $M\delta^*\le(1-t)^{-1}\delta^*$.
Nonpositive objectives require no bound beyond $0\le\delta^*$.
Taking the supremum proves
\eqref{eq:all-moment-actual-ideal-comparison}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Actual favorable models with sparse repair]
\label{thm:all-moment-actual-prime-models}
Fix the finite character portfolio and an integer $k\ge2$.
Put
\[
 \theta_k=1-\frac1{2k},\qquad d=k-1,\qquad
 C_k=16\zeta(2)B_d^2.
\]
There is a fixed $J_0(k,\mathcal Q)$ with the following
properties. For each $j\ge J_0$, $u=2^j$, partition $[u,2u)$
into cells of length $h=2^{\lfloor\theta_kj\rfloor}$.
On every cell choose nonnegative actual prime weights
minimizing
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 E_{I,k}=\min\sum_{p\in S_I}(w_{p,k}-A(p))_+,\\
 \sum_{p\in S_I}p^r w_{p,k}
                 =R\int_I x^r\,d\nu(x)\quad(0\le r<k).
 \end{gathered}
 \label{eq:higher-moment-prime-program}
\end{equation}
A minimizer with at most $k$ positive-overweight coordinates
exists. Choose the lexicographically least such minimizer
in increasing prime order, and put $w_{p,k}=0$ below
$2^{J_0}$. This defines the model before any RH assumption.

Its full actual prime Euler product has an unconditional
continuation
\begin{equation}
 F_{k,\mathcal Q}(s)=\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-w_{p,k}}
       =\left(\frac{s}{s-1}\right)^R e^{G_k(s)},
       \qquad \Re s>1/2,
 \label{eq:higher-moment-favorable-product}
\end{equation}
where $G_k$ is holomorphic. The product initially means its
full Euler logarithm on $\Re s>1$.

Let $e_{p,k}=(w_{p,k}-A(p))_+$,
$\mathcal B_k=\{p:e_{p,k}>0\}$, and
$E_{j,k}=\sum_{u\le p<2u}e_{p,k}$. Then, unconditionally,
\begin{equation}
 \#(\mathcal B_k\cap[u,2u))\le k\,u/h
                           \le2k\,u^{1/(2k)}.
 \label{eq:sparse-prime-repair-count}
\end{equation}
For the ideal degree-$(k-1)$ defects on the original cells,
write $\Delta_{j,k}^*=\sum_I\delta_{I,k}^*$. With the same
fixed prime-gap constants as above, put
\[
 \epsilon_{j,k}=C_g(2u)^{2/3}/h.
\]
The starting threshold can be chosen so $C_k\epsilon_{j,k}\le1/2$,
and then
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 \Delta_{j,k}^*\le E_{j,k}
              \le(1-C_k\epsilon_{j,k})^{-1}\Delta_{j,k}^*,\\
 E_{j,k}\le(1+2C_k\epsilon_{j,k})\Delta_{j,k}^*,
 \qquad \epsilon_{j,k}\ll_k u^{2/3-\theta_k}=o(1).
 \end{gathered}
 \label{eq:higher-moment-full-capacity-consumer}
\end{equation}
Every reference zero with real part $\beta>1/2$ forces
\begin{equation}
 \limsup_{j\to\infty}
       \frac{\log(1+E_{j,k})}{j\log2}\ge\beta.
 \label{eq:sparse-repair-zero-signal}
\end{equation}
In particular, for every $\epsilon>0$, infinitely many repair
primes satisfy
\begin{equation}
 e_{p,k}\ge p^{\,\beta-1/(2k)-\epsilon}.
 \label{eq:sparse-repair-heavy-atom}
\end{equation}
All constants, thresholds and choices here may depend on
the fixed $k$ and the fixed portfolio; $k$ and conductors
do not grow with $j$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
On a cell in $[u,2u]$, the density of $R\nu$ lies between
$R\ell_u$ and $R/\log u$, with ratio at most $4\zeta(2)$.
The prime-gap argument in
Theorem~\ref{thm:actual-favorable-prime-model} gives covering
radius at most $g_u=C_g(2u)^{2/3}$ using actual primes inside
the cell. This includes both end strips. Since
\[
 \epsilon_{j,k}\le2C_g2^{2/3}u^{2/3-\theta_k}\longrightarrow0,
\]
choose $J_0\ge3$ with $2^{J_0}>2p_0+2$ and
$2C_kC_g2^{2/3}2^{J_0(2/3-\theta_k)}\le1/2$.
These inequalities hold for all later blocks; they ensure
the grid exists inside each cell and the norming hypothesis
holds. The cell endpoints are even integers greater than
$2$, so they carry no prime atoms. The original half-open
partition and its closed hulls therefore use identical
prime nodes and continuous moments.

The norming lemma places the normalized target moment vector
in the convex hull of actual prime moment vectors. It gives
feasibility of \eqref{eq:higher-moment-prime-program}.
The second lemma gives attainment, sparse attainment and the
ideal comparison, uniformly on all cells. The set of optimal
weights with at most $k$ positive-overweight coordinates is
a finite union of closed subsets of the compact optimal set:
outside a chosen set of at most $k$ coordinates require
$w_p\le A(p)$. Successive coordinate minima therefore define
the announced tie rule. Summing the cell comparisons and
counting the cells proves
\eqref{eq:sparse-prime-repair-count} and
\eqref{eq:higher-moment-full-capacity-consumer}.

Every block has total weight
$W_j=R\nu([u,2u))\le Ru/\log u$, ensuring initial absolute
convergence on $\Re s>1$. Retain the complete normalization:
\begin{align}
 G_k(s)&=-R\int_1^{2^{J_0}}f_s(x)\,d\nu(x)
                                      +\sum_{j\ge J_0}G_{j,k}(s),\notag\\
 G_{j,k}(s)&=\sum_{I\subset[u,2u)}
    \left[\sum_{p\in S_I}w_{p,k}f_s(p)
                          -R\int_I f_s(x)\,d\nu(x)\right],
 \qquad u=2^j .
 \label{eq:higher-moment-complete-logarithm}
\end{align}
Here $f_s(x)=-\log(1-x^{-s})$ retains every prime power.
The moments cancel every Taylor term of degree less than
$k$. The full remainder estimate from
Theorem~\ref{thm:long-interval-reverse-capacity} gives
\begin{equation}
 |G_{j,k}(s)|\le
 \frac{2R}{k\log2}
       \left(\prod_{r=0}^{k-1}(|s|+r)\right)
       \frac{u^{1/2-\Re s}}{(1-u^{-\Re s})^k}.
 \label{eq:higher-moment-prime-error}
\end{equation}
Indeed $h^k\le u^{k-1/2}$, and the model and the actual
quadrature have equal positive mass. This bound converges
normally on $\Re s>1/2$. The retained finite initial integral
is holomorphic on $\Re s>0$, with an integrable logarithmic
endpoint at $1$. Exponentiating the initial exact identity
proves \eqref{eq:higher-moment-favorable-product}.

Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:one-sided-prime-model-transfer} to
this independently favorable model. If the limsup in
\eqref{eq:sparse-repair-zero-signal} were below $\beta$,
choose $\eta$ strictly between their maximum with $1/2$
and $\beta$. The dyadic bounds would give cumulative
overweight $O(X^\eta)$, excluding that reference zero.
This proves the signal statement without assuming RH.
For the last claim choose arbitrarily late blocks with
$E_{j,k}>u^{\beta-\epsilon/3}$ and use
\eqref{eq:sparse-prime-repair-count}. Some repair weight
is at least $(2k)^{-1}u^{\beta-1/(2k)-\epsilon/3}$.
For large blocks this is at least
$p^{\beta-1/(2k)-\epsilon}$, since $u\le p<2u$.
The chosen primes tend to infinity.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[An RH equivalence for predefined sparse repairs]
\label{cor:predefined-sparse-repair-RH}
Take $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$ and construct all the above models
unconditionally. Let $\mathcal K$ be any unbounded subset
of $\{2,3,\ldots\}$. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
\item RH.
\item For every fixed $k\in\mathcal K$ and $\epsilon>0$,
\begin{equation}
 E_{j,k}\ll_{k,\epsilon}
                u^{1/2+1/(2k)+\epsilon},\qquad u=2^j.
 \label{eq:sparse-repair-RH-total}
\end{equation}
\item For every fixed $k\in\mathcal K$ and $\epsilon>0$,
\begin{equation}
 \max_{\substack{u\le p<2u\\p\ {\rm prime}}}e_{p,k}
          \ll_{k,\epsilon}u^{1/2+1/(2k)+\epsilon}.
 \label{eq:sparse-repair-RH-individual}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
An empty maximum is defined as zero. The ideal defects
$\Delta_{j,k}^*$ may replace $E_{j,k}$ in the second condition.
The quantifiers fix $k$ before taking $j\to\infty$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
First compare the reference counting model with the usual
logarithmic integral $\operatorname{Li}_2(X)=\int_2^Xdt/\log t$.
For $r\ge1$, the integral test gives
\[
 0\le1-\zeta(r+1)^{-1}
       \le\zeta(r+1)-1
       \le2^{-r}(1/2+1/r)\le(3/2)2^{-r}.
\]
In the defining series of $\nu$, sum this bound with
$t=\log x$. For $x\ge2$ it gives
\begin{equation}
 0\le\frac1{\log x}-\nu'(x)
       \le\frac{x^{-1}+(3/2)x^{-1/2}}{\log x}
       \le\frac{5x^{-1/2}}{2\log x}.
 \label{eq:continuous-model-logarithmic-integral-error}
\end{equation}
Integration shows
\[
 0\le\operatorname{Li}_2(X)-\nu([2,X])
       \le\frac5{\log2}(\sqrt X-\sqrt2).
\]
Thus the model-count error is $O(\sqrt X)$, unconditionally.

Assume RH. Its classical consequence
$\pi(X)-\operatorname{li}(X)=O(\sqrt X\log X)$ is used
only in this forward direction; see
\cite[Eq.~6.16.5]{DLMFPrimeError}.
The preceding model estimate shows, uniformly over every
interval $J\subset[u,2u]$ and every endpoint convention,
\[
 \bigl(\nu(J)-\#\{p\in J\}\bigr)_+
                                \ll\sqrt u\log u.
\]
One-sided limits of the same cumulative estimate handle
prime endpoints; no boundary prime is silently discarded.
There are $u/h\le2u^{1/(2k)}$ cells. The ideal defect bound
$\delta_{I,k}^*\le\lceil k/2\rceil d_I$, followed by the
actual comparison
\eqref{eq:higher-moment-full-capacity-consumer}, gives the
stronger estimate
\begin{equation}
 E_{j,k}\ll_k u^{1/2+1/(2k)}\log u.
 \label{eq:RH-implies-sparse-repair-rate}
\end{equation}
This proves the second condition and hence the third,
since every individual overweight is at most its block sum.

Conversely, a failure of RH gives a zero with real part
$\beta>1/2$, by the functional equation. Under the second
condition choose one fixed $k\in\mathcal K$ with
$1/2+1/(2k)<\beta$, and then a sufficiently small fixed
$\epsilon>0$. Its rate contradicts
\eqref{eq:sparse-repair-zero-signal}.
Under the third condition use the sparse repair count to
obtain
\[
 E_{j,k}\ll_{k,\epsilon}u^{1/2+1/k+\epsilon}.
\]
Choose instead one fixed $k\in\mathcal K$ with
$1/2+1/k<\beta$ and then small $\epsilon$; the same
unconditional signal statement gives a contradiction.
Finally \eqref{eq:higher-moment-full-capacity-consumer}
compares the actual and ideal defects by a factor tending
to $1$, proving the stated replacement.
\end{proof}

For clarity, the full positively weighted model and its
repair set have different sizes. If
$\mathcal S_k=\{p:w_{p,k}>0\}$ and
$A_*=\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}\varphi(q)>0$, the exact identity
and its cardinality consequence are
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{u\le p<2u}\min\{w_{p,k},A(p)\}=W_j-E_{j,k},
 \qquad
 \#(\mathcal S_k\cap[u,2u))
          \ge\frac{(W_j-E_{j,k})_+}{A_*}.
 \label{eq:full-carrier-versus-sparse-repair}
\end{equation}
The unconditional density comparison just proved gives
$W_j=(R+o(1))u/\log u$. Therefore an independent overweight
bound with any fixed exponent less than $1$ forces at least
$(R/A_*+o(1))u/\log u$ selected primes per block.
For $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$ the classical prime number theorem
then makes the full carrier density one among primes.
Meanwhile the repair set has only $O_k(u^{1/(2k)})$ primes
per block. A small unweighted repair count therefore does
not exclude a zero: its weights can be large, as
\eqref{eq:sparse-repair-heavy-atom} quantifies.

These are ordinary proofs for independently defined actual
prime models, not yet Lean formalizations of the polynomial
norming, finite moment duality or Euler continuation.
The already compiled positive-Dirichlet continuation and
capped support-function lemmas remain inputs to that work.
No unconditional power estimate for $E_{j,k}$ has been
proved here; in particular the certified reference boundary
remains $7/8$. No literature priority is asserted.


\paragraph{Exact contact geometry of the optimized carrier.}
The finite optimum has an explicit polynomial description,
retaining the mass deficit and every signed capacity term.

\begin{theorem}[Binary interpolation and the contact-prime profile]
\label{thm:prime-repair-contact-profile}
Fix a cell in the all-moment construction. Write
$\mu=R\nu|_I$, $S=S_I$, $H=\mu(I)$, $C=\sum_{p\in S}A(p)$,
and $E=E_{I,k}$. For $\deg P<k$ put
\[
 \mathcal D(P)=\int_I P\,d\mu-\sum_{p\in S}A(p)(P(p))_+.
\]
There are at least $k$ actual prime nodes in $S$.
For each $k$-element subset $T\subset S$ and map
$b:T\to\{0,1\}$, form the unique degree-less-than-$k$
Lagrange interpolant $P_{T,b}$. Retain those satisfying
$P_{T,b}(p)\le1$ at every node in $S$, and call the resulting
finite nonempty set $\mathfrak P_{k,S}$. Then
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 E=\max_{P\in\mathfrak P_{k,S}}\mathcal D(P)\\
 =\max\left(\{0,H-C\}\cup
 \{\mathcal D(P):P\in\mathfrak P_{k,S},\
                                    P\text{ nonconstant}\}\right).
 \end{gathered}
 \label{eq:binary-prime-interpolation-dual}
\end{equation}
Let $P$ be any maximizing feasible dual polynomial and $w$
any optimal actual weight vector. The complete contact rules are
\begin{equation}
 \begin{array}{c|c}
 \text{dual value}&\text{optimal actual weight}\\ \hline
 P(p)<0&w_p=0\\
 P(p)=0&0\le w_p\le A(p)\\
 0<P(p)<1&w_p=A(p)\\
 P(p)=1&w_p\ge A(p).
 \end{array}
 \label{eq:prime-repair-contact-rules}
\end{equation}
If $E>\max\{0,H-C\}$, every maximizing dual polynomial is
nonconstant. There are then at most $k-1$ repair primes
$w_p>A(p)$, at most $k-1$ partly used nodes $0<w_p<A(p)$,
and at most $2(k-1)$ nodes with weight different from both
$0$ and $A(p)$.
If $E=H-C>0$, the constant polynomial $P=1$ is optimal and
every optimal vector has $w_p\ge A(p)$ at all nodes.
If $E=0$, every optimal vector has $w_p\le A(p)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
We first justify dual attainment. If $E=0$, the zero polynomial
attains it. If $E>0$, take a maximizing sequence with
$\mathcal D(P)\ge E/2$. Its polynomials have positive
$\mu$-integral and node values at most $1$. The proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:positive-objective-prime-norming}, with
$t=C_k\epsilon_{j,k}<1$, gives
\[
 \max_I P\le(1-t)^{-1},\qquad
 \max_I|P|\le4B_{k-1}(r/\ell)(1-t)^{-1}.
\]
The constants here concern this fixed cell.
Interpolation on the fixed equispaced grid bounds every
coefficient. The feasible inequalities and
$\mathcal D(P)\ge E/2$ are closed. Compactness in the
finite-dimensional coefficient space therefore supplies
a limit attaining $E$.

The actual nodes are unisolvent for degree less than $k$.
If $Q$ of that degree vanishes at every node, a feasible
actual quadrature gives $\int_I Q\,d\mu=0$.
For every real $v$, the polynomial $1+vQ$ has integral
$H>0$ and node values $1$. Norming bounds its maximum on
$I$ by $(1-t)^{-1}$, independently of $v$.
Varying both signs of $v$ forces $Q=0$ on $I$, hence
identically. If $|S|<k$, the nonzero polynomial
$\prod_{p\in S}(x-p)$ contradicts this fact.
Thus $|S|\ge k$.

Start with an optimal dual polynomial.
If fewer than $k$ distinct nodes have value $0$ or $1$,
choose a nonzero polynomial $Q$ of degree less than $k$
vanishing at all those contacts.
For sufficiently small positive and negative $v$,
$P+vQ$ stays feasible and keeps every noncontact node's
sign. The objective is affine on that sign region;
maximality in both directions makes its slope zero.
Unisolvence supplies a noncontact node where $Q\ne0$.
Orient $v$ so its value increases, and move to the first
time a noncontact node reaches $0$ or $1$.
That time is positive and finite: the selected node
must reach $0$ if it started negative or $1$ if positive.
Until then all signs and node upper bounds are preserved.
The objective stays constant, all old contacts stay fixed,
and a new contact appears. Repeat until at least $k$
distinct contacts are present. Their binary values
determine $P$ by Lagrange interpolation.
This proves the finite maximum. Constant interpolants
$0$ and $1$ supply the explicit tests $0$ and $H-C$.

For the weight classification put $P_+=\max(P,0)$,
$P_-=\max(-P,0)$ and $e=(w-A)_+$ at a node.
The exact signed slack identity is
\begin{equation}
 e-(wP-A P_+)=(1-P_+)e+P_+(A-w)_++P_-w.
 \label{eq:overweight-three-signed-slacks}
\end{equation}
All three terms on the right are nonnegative for $w\ge0$,
$P\le1$. Moment matching and primal-dual equality give
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_{p\in S}\bigl[(1-P(p)_+)e_p
        &+P(p)_+(A(p)-w_p)_++P(p)_-w_p\bigr]\\
        &=E-\mathcal D(P)=0.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:zero-total-prime-contact-slack}
\end{equation}
Thus every node gap vanishes. If $P<0$, its last term
forces $w=0$; if $P=0$, its first forces $w\le A$.
For $0<P<1$, the first two force both positive parts
of $w-A$ to vanish, so $w=A$.
For $P=1$, the second forces $w\ge A$.
No absolute-value estimate of the signed arithmetic
main terms enters these conclusions.

A constant feasible polynomial has objective at most
$\max\{0,H-C\}$: for $c\le0$ it is $cH\le0$,
and for $0\le c\le1$ it is $c(H-C)$.
The strict non-mass-deficit case therefore has only
nonconstant optimizers. Each of $P=0$ and $P=1$ has
at most $k-1$ distinct roots; the contact rules give
all three exceptional-node counts.
When $E=H-C>0$, the constant optimizer $1$ instead
forces $w\ge A$ at every node.
When $E=0$, all nonnegative excesses vanish.
\end{proof}

Apart from contact primes, the optimized carrier therefore
uses either the full available capacity or none of it.
For $k=2$, the nonconstant interpolation candidates are
affine, recovering the actual-hull finite-strike description.
For larger fixed $k$ the candidate list is still finite
and uses the actual prime locations.
Estimating its signed objectives, including $H-C$, remains
the separate arithmetic task.

The exact signed decomposition, its nonnegativity, the
summed primal-dual gap, vanishing of every optimal node
gap, and the four contact implications have maintained
Lean proofs in
\texttt{Proof/}\allowbreak
\texttt{PrimeClassDeletion/}\allowbreak
\texttt{OverweightSlack.lean}.
They use only \texttt{propext}, \texttt{Classical.choice}
and \texttt{Quot.sound}, and are exported by the
prime-class-deletion assembly.
Polynomial-dual attainment, finite interpolation and
the root-count consequences above retain their full
ordinary proofs here. This classification supplies
no new weight estimate or certified improvement of $7/8$.


\subsubsection{A computable detector with dyadic rational weights}
\label{sec:computable-rational-prime-repairs}

The preceding real optimization can be replaced by a finite
algorithm using rational data. The algorithm needs no unknown
prime-gap starting threshold, and keeps the sparse repair support.

We first record the quantitative interpolation input now checked
in Lean. For $N\ge1$ distinct nodes $v_i\in[0,1]$, a polynomial
$\deg P<N$ with $|P(v_i)|\le L$, $L\ge0$, and node separation at least
$\delta>0$, the maintained proof gives
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 |P'(x)|&\le L\sum_{i=1}^N
       \frac{N-1}{\left|\prod_{j\ne i}(v_i-v_j)\right|}\\
       &\le L\frac{N(N-1)}{\delta^{N-1}}
                         \qquad(0\le x\le1).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:checked-node-separation-derivative}
\end{equation}
Every denominator is nonzero. The same owner proves exact
interpolation and its differentiated finite-product formula,
using cached polynomial-root and derivative APIs. Its fifteen
theorem declarations have standard-axiom audits. The owner is
\texttt{Proof/}\allowbreak
\texttt{PrimeClassDeletion/}\allowbreak
\texttt{PolynomialPrimeNorming.lean}.
The integral norming argument and the rational detector theorem
below remain ordinary proofs.

\begin{theorem}[A computable sparse rational repair detector]
\label{thm:computable-rational-repair-detector}
Fix $k\ge2$ and the finite character portfolio. Put
$d=k-1$, $B_d=2^d d^{d+1}/d!$ and
\[
 C_k^{\rm alg}=32B_d^2,\qquad
 K_k=64B_d\,k(2d)^d.
\]
The following finite algorithm defines nonnegative dyadic rational
weights $\widehat w_{p,k}$ on actual primes.

Start at $j=3$. On $[u,2u)$, $u=2^j$, use the previous cells
of length $h=2^{\lfloor(1-1/(2k))j\rfloor}$. For each cell
$I=[a,b)$ enumerate its actual primes $S$. If $S$ is empty,
declare it bad. Otherwise compute its closed-hull covering radius
\[
 r_I=\max\left\{p_{\min}-a,\ b-p_{\max},\
             \frac12\max_{\text{consecutive }p,p'\in S}(p'-p)\right\},
\]
where the last maximum is zero for a singleton.
Declare the cell good exactly when
$C_k^{\rm alg}r_I/h\le1/2$. On bad cells put all weights zero.

On a good cell, write $y_p=(p-a)/h$, $\tau=u^{-3}$,
$M=u^4$, and compute rational numbers $\widetilde m_r$ with
certified errors
\begin{equation}
 \left|\widetilde m_r-
       R\int_I\left(\frac{x-a}{h}\right)^r d\nu(x)\right|
                         \le\tau \qquad(0\le r<k).
 \label{eq:rational-model-target-precision}
\end{equation}
Search the finite set of vectors
$v_p\in M^{-1}\mathbb Z\cap[0,2Ru]$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 \left|\sum_{p\in S}v_p y_p^r-\widetilde m_r\right|
                              \le2\tau\quad(0\le r<k),\\
 \#\{p\in S:v_p>A(p)\}\le k.
 \end{gathered}
 \label{eq:rational-model-finite-search}
\end{equation}
This set is nonempty. Minimize the rational objective
$\sum_{p\in S}(v_p-A(p))_+$ and break ties lexicographically
in increasing prime order. Its unique output defines
$\widehat w_{p,k}$. Put all weights below $8$ equal to zero.

This algorithm terminates for each prime and uses only finite
prime enumeration, rational comparisons and certified evaluation
of the positive model moments. Its full Euler product satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \widehat F_{k,\mathcal Q}(s)
   =\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-\widehat w_{p,k}}
   =\left(\frac{s}{s-1}\right)^R e^{\widehat G_k(s)}
                       \qquad(\Re s>1/2),
 \label{eq:rational-model-favorable-product}
\end{equation}
with $\widehat G_k$ holomorphic, unconditionally.

Set $\widehat e_{p,k}=(\widehat w_{p,k}-A(p))_+$,
$\widehat{\mathcal B}_k=\{p:\widehat e_{p,k}>0\}$, and
$\widehat E_{j,k}=\sum_{u\le p<2u}\widehat e_{p,k}$.
For every $j\ge3$,
\begin{equation}
 \#(\widehat{\mathcal B}_k\cap[u,2u))
                              \le2k\,u^{1/(2k)}.
 \label{eq:rational-model-sparse-repair-count}
\end{equation}
For all sufficiently large $j$, the exact real optimum from
Theorem~\ref{thm:all-moment-actual-prime-models} obeys
\begin{equation}
 0\le E_{j,k}-\widehat E_{j,k}
                \le6K_k\,u^{-3+1/(2k)}.
 \label{eq:rational-real-overweight-comparison}
\end{equation}
Thus rationalization preserves its dyadic growth exponent.
For $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$, and any unbounded set $\mathcal K$
of fixed integers $k\ge2$,
\begin{equation}
 {\rm RH}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \widehat E_{j,k}\ll_{k,\epsilon}
             u^{1/2+1/(2k)+\epsilon}
       \quad\text{for every }k\in\mathcal K,\ \epsilon>0 .
 \label{eq:computable-rational-repair-RH}
\end{equation}
The analogous individual-overweight criterion from
Corollary~\ref{cor:predefined-sparse-repair-RH} holds as well.
Every reference zero with real part $\beta>1/2$ forces the
rational repair mass to have dyadic limsup exponent at least
$\beta$, and forces infinitely many
$\widehat e_{p,k}\ge p^{\beta-1/(2k)-\epsilon}$ for every
$\epsilon>0$. These are fixed-$k$, fixed-portfolio assertions.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
All grid tests are exact rational tests. Here $B_d$ is rational,
the endpoints and prime locations are integers, and $r_I$ is
rational. On the cell the density ratio of $R\nu$ is at most
$4\zeta(2)<8$. Hence the good-cell test implies
\[
 4B_d^2(r/\ell)(r_I/h)\le1/2.
\]
The positive-objective norming and actual quadrature results
therefore apply on every good cell, even at a scale where
a nonexplicit prime-gap threshold is unavailable.

For completeness the target moments admit certified rational
approximations uniformly in their finite input data.
The series defining $\nu'$ has coefficients
$1/\zeta(n+1)$, $n\ge1$. The defining Dirichlet series for
$\zeta(n+1)$ has a rational partial sum and remainder at most
$T^{-n}/n$ after the integer cutoff $T$; its reciprocal can
be enclosed because $\zeta(n+1)\ge1$.
On a fixed cell choose an integer $U>\log b$.
For $N\ge2U$, the exponential-series tail is bounded by
\[
 \sum_{n>N}\frac{U^n}{n!}
                  \le\frac{2U^{N+1}}{(N+1)!}.
\]
It controls the defining density series uniformly on the
cell. Finite partial sums have computable derivative bounds
there, since $a\ge8$. Rational interval Riemann sums, with
certified elementary logarithms and the displayed tail,
therefore enclose each normalized moment to arbitrarily
small error. Taking a rational midpoint once its enclosing
interval has width at most $2\tau$ gives
\eqref{eq:rational-model-target-precision}.
No decision about an equality of unknown real numbers enters
this procedure.

Let $w$ be an exact sparse optimal quadrature on a good cell.
Its total mass is $H=R\nu(I)<2Ru$, so every coordinate is
within the finite search bound. Round it down:
\[
 v_p=M^{-1}\lfloor Mw_p\rfloor,\qquad
                  0\le w_p-v_p<M^{-1}.
\]
There are at most $u$ prime nodes in the cell and
$0\le y_p\le1$. Thus each normalized moment changes by
at most $u/M=\tau$. Combining this with the target enclosure
makes $v$ feasible for \eqref{eq:rational-model-finite-search}.
Rounding down creates no new repair primes and never increases
the positive-overweight objective. This proves both feasibility
and the cellwise bound
$\widehat E_{I,k}\le E_{I,k}$.
The finite minimization and tie rule are consequently effective.
Every chosen output, not just this rounded witness, satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \left|\sum_{p\in S}\widehat w_{p,k}y_p^r-m_r\right|
                \le3\tau\qquad(0\le r<k),
 \label{eq:rational-model-actual-moment-error}
\end{equation}
where $m_r$ denotes the true normalized moment.
Its mass is in particular at most $H+3\tau$.

We next bound the optimization loss. If $E_{I,k}=0$, its
rational optimum is also zero. Otherwise take an attaining
optimal dual polynomial, expressed in the normalized coordinate
as $Q(y)=\sum_{r<k}c_r y^r$. The positive-objective norming
proof and the good-cell test give
\[
 \max_{[0,1]}|Q|\le
       4B_d(r/\ell)(1-1/2)^{-1}\le64B_d.
\]
Interpolate at $0,1/d,\ldots,1$.
Each denominator product has absolute value at least $d^{-d}$,
and each numerator product has coefficient absolute sum at most
$2^d$: the coefficient sum of a product is at most the product
of coefficient sums, and each factor $y-j/d$ contributes at
most $2$. Summing the $d+1=k$ basis terms gives the explicit bound
\begin{equation}
 \sum_{r<k}|c_r|\le
       64B_d\,k(2d)^d=K_k .
 \label{eq:rational-model-dual-coefficient-budget}
\end{equation}
This is an auxiliary finite-dimensional conditioning bound;
the signed prime-capacity objective itself is retained.

The checked node weak-duality inequality, summed over the
actual finite support, now gives
\begin{align*}
 \widehat E_{I,k}
 &\ge\sum_{p\in S}\widehat w_{p,k}Q(y_p)
                         -\sum_{p\in S}A(p)Q(y_p)_+\\
 &=E_{I,k}+
       \sum_{r<k}c_r
          \left(\sum_{p\in S}\widehat w_{p,k}y_p^r-m_r\right)\\
 &\ge E_{I,k}-3K_k\tau .
\end{align*}
All constant and nonconstant dual candidates, including the
mass-deficit row, are covered. Together with the preceding
upper bound this proves the cell comparison.

The classical weak prime-gap estimate makes all cells good
after some finite scale: their covering radii are
$O(u^{2/3})$ and $h\ge u^{1-1/(2k)}/2$.
The algorithm does not need to know that scale.
There are $u/h\le2u^{1/(2k)}$ cells, so summing the comparison
after that scale proves
\eqref{eq:rational-real-overweight-comparison}.
The imposed repair-count constraint, including zero weights
on bad cells, proves \eqref{eq:rational-model-sparse-repair-count}
at every scale where the algorithm runs.

It remains to prove favorability for the chosen algorithmic
output. Small moment residuals do not imply proximity in
total variation to a particular exact optimizer; we instead
compare its full Euler logarithm directly with the continuous
model. Let $\mathcal H_k$ be the union of $(1,8)$ and all bad
cells. There are only finitely many bad cells, so this is
a bounded union, with the initial endpoint at $1$ retained.
Every block has actual mass at most
$R\nu([u,2u))+3\tau u/h\ll_Ru/\log u$.
Thus the initial full Euler logarithm converges absolutely
on $\Re s>1$, where the exact identity is
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \log\widehat F_{k,\mathcal Q}(s)-R\log M(s)
 &=-R\int_{\mathcal H_k}f_s(x)\,d\nu(x)
                         +\sum_{j\ge3}\widehat G_{j,k}(s),\\
 \widehat G_{j,k}(s)
 &=\sum_{\substack{I\subset[u,2u)\\I\ {\rm good}}}
       \left[\sum_{p\in S_I}\widehat w_{p,k}f_s(p)
                              -R\int_I f_s(x)\,d\nu(x)\right].
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:rational-model-complete-logarithm}
\end{equation}
Every prime power is retained in $f_s$.

Fix a compact set in $\Re s>1/2$, and let
$\sigma_0=\inf\Re s>1/2$ on that set.
Expand $f_s(a+hy)$ through degree $k-1$.
Its coefficient of $y^r$ is
$h^r f_s^{(r)}(a)/r!=O_{k,\mathrm{compact}}(u^{-\sigma_0})$,
including $r=0$. The full Euler-derivative estimate used
above proves this uniformly, since $h/u\le1$.
By \eqref{eq:rational-model-actual-moment-error}, the surviving
Taylor polynomial contributes at most
$O_{k,\mathrm{compact}}(\tau u^{1/(2k)-\sigma_0})$ per block.
The model and actual positive masses in the remainder
sum to at most $2R\nu([u,2u))+3\tau u/h$.
The same order-$k$ Taylor remainder, using
$h^k\le u^{k-1/2}$, consequently gives
\begin{equation}
 |\widehat G_{j,k}(s)|\ll_{k,R,\mathrm{compact}}
       \frac{u^{1/2-\sigma_0}}{\log u}
                         +u^{-3+1/(2k)-\sigma_0}.
 \label{eq:rational-model-summable-error}
\end{equation}
Both terms are summable over dyadic blocks.
The finite integral over $\mathcal H_k$ is holomorphic on
$\Re s>0$, with the same integrable logarithmic endpoint
at $1$ as before.
Thus \eqref{eq:rational-model-complete-logarithm} defines
$\widehat G_k$ holomorphically on $\Re s>1/2$.
Exponentiation proves
\eqref{eq:rational-model-favorable-product}, including its
unchanged pole order $R$.

Finally the exact/rational cost comparison transfers the
previous RH equivalence. Alternatively, the reverse implication
and bad-zero readout follow directly from the independently
favorable rational model and the one-sided positive quotient.
Its sparse count gives the individual-weight criterion and
the heavy-atom consequence exactly as before.
RH is used only for the forward arithmetic estimate.
\end{proof}

This supplies an actual computable prime set
$\widehat{\mathcal B}_k$ with computable dyadic rational weights.
Its weighted signal reaches every $\beta>1/2$.
The finite algorithm defines the observable at every scale;
running it on a finite collection of scales is not a proof
of its eventual RH-equivalent bound.
The real and rational mass exponents agree, but neither
construction supplies the independent power estimate.

The relation to modified Euler products is structural:
Grosswald--Schnitzer~\cite{GrosswaldSchnitzer} modify Euler
bases, whereas the construction here fixes the actual primes
and chooses effective rational exponents with a controlled
one-sided capacity cost. No literature priority is asserted.
The rational construction, coefficient budget and Euler
continuation above are ordinary full proofs. The fifteen
interpolation/derivative theorems and the earlier signed
weak-duality/contact core are separately Lean checked.
The certified reference boundary remains $7/8$.

\begin{corollary}[Rational linear programming gives certified repair intervals]
\label{cor:rational-LP-prime-repairs}
On a good cell of Theorem~\ref{thm:computable-rational-repair-detector},
solve the bounded rational linear program
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 E_I^{\rm LP}=\min\sum_{p\in S}e_p,\qquad
 0\le v_p\le2Ru,\quad0\le e_p\le2Ru,\quad e_p\ge v_p-A(p),\\
 \left|\sum_{p\in S}v_p y_p^r-\widetilde m_r\right|
                                      \le\tau\quad(0\le r<k).
 \end{gathered}
 \label{eq:rational-repair-linear-program}
\end{equation}
It has a rational optimum obtainable by finite exact linear
algebra. Rational conic compression followed by rounding down
to multiples of $M^{-1}$ produces weights
$\widehat w_{p,k}^{\rm LP}$ with at most $k$ repair primes,
the same moment tolerances as
\eqref{eq:rational-model-finite-search}, and costs satisfying
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 E_I^{\rm LP}\le E_{I,k}\le E_I^{\rm LP}+2K_k\tau,\\
 E_I^{\rm LP}-k/M\le\widehat E_{I,k}^{\rm LP}
                                      \le E_I^{\rm LP}.
 \end{gathered}
 \label{eq:rational-LP-repair-certificates}
\end{equation}
Thus this procedure gives both a rational interval containing
the exact real optimum and an actual sparse dyadic model.
Its full favorable continuation, zero readout and RH criterion
are those of the preceding theorem. For all sufficiently large
blocks its complete error is bounded by
\begin{equation}
 0\le E_{j,k}-\widehat E_{j,k}^{\rm LP}
       \le4K_k u^{-3+1/(2k)}+2k u^{-4+1/(2k)}.
 \label{eq:rational-LP-total-repair-error}
\end{equation}
The LP output need not minimize the original dyadic grid problem;
the displayed certificates are sufficient for every conclusion.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
An exact real quadrature $w$ and its excess $(w-A)_+$ give a
feasible point of \eqref{eq:rational-repair-linear-program},
since the target error is at most $\tau$.
Both variable boxes are valid because $w_p\le H<2Ru$ and
$A(p)\ge0$. The feasible polytope is nonempty and compact,
with rational coefficients. It has an optimal vertex.
At a vertex the active constraint normals span the ambient
space; otherwise a nonzero common null direction would allow
small feasible moves in both directions.
A full-rank active subsystem therefore determines that vertex
by rational Gaussian elimination. Enumerating such subsystems
is one terminating exact solver. This proves rational attainment
without comparing unknown real moments.

At an optimum $e_p=(v_p-A(p))_+$: any strict excess slack can
be decreased without changing the moment constraints.
Clip $v$ to $a_p=\min(v_p,A(p))$. Its rational excess vector
$e$ has moment vector
\[
 \sum_{p\in S}e_p(1,y_p,\ldots,y_p^{k-1}).
\]
While more than $k$ excess coordinates are positive, rational
Gaussian elimination gives a nonzero rational dependence among
their moment columns. The first coordinate makes its
coefficients sum to zero, so they have both signs.
Move along the dependence by the least positive ratio needed
to make one weight vanish. Nonnegativity, every moment and
the total excess mass are preserved. The support shrinks,
so the process terminates with a rational $e'$ on at most
$k$ actual nodes.

The vector $v'=a+e'$ has exactly the original LP moments.
Its total mass is at most $H+2\tau<2Ru$: use $u\ge8$,
$H\le Ru/\log8<Ru$ and $2\tau<Ru$.
Thus it respects every coordinate box. Its actual overweight
is at most $\sum e'=E_I^{\rm LP}$. It is itself LP feasible,
so optimality forces equality. In particular its repairs are
supported on the at most $k$ nodes of $e'$.

The true normalized moment errors of this compressed optimizer
are at most $2\tau$. The attaining exact polynomial dual,
with coefficient budget $K_k$, therefore gives
$E_I^{\rm LP}\ge E_{I,k}-2K_k\tau$ by the same complete signed
weak-duality calculation used above.
The reverse inequality follows from the feasible exact
quadrature. This proves the first certificate.

Round $v'$ down on the $M^{-1}$ grid.
Every normalized moment changes by at most $u/M=\tau$,
so its discrepancy from the rational targets is at most
$2\tau$, and from the true moments at most $3\tau$.
No repair coordinate is created. Only the at most $k$
existing repairs can decrease the positive-overweight cost,
each by less than $M^{-1}$. This proves the second certificate.
Summing these cell bounds, with $u/h\le2u^{1/(2k)}$,
proves \eqref{eq:rational-LP-total-repair-error} after all cells
are good. On bad cells use zero weights as before.

Finally the analytic proof above uses only nonnegative
weights, these normalized moment tolerances, the mass bound,
and the sparse repair count; it does not require a dyadic
grid minimizer. All its continuation and zero-signal
conclusions therefore apply to this LP construction.
This is an ordinary proof of the rational optimization and
compression procedure; those steps have not yet been
formalized in Lean.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Sparse repairs as a prime-Poisson overlap criterion}
\label{sec:prime-poisson-repair-RH}

There is a probability interpretation of the computable repair
weights which retains their unconditional zero signal.
The probability space here is an auxiliary product of Poisson
coordinates indexed by prime powers. It is constructed from
the weights, rather than asserted to be the sampling law of a
polynomial or affine family. The positive floor below is needed:
an unregularized zero weight would give a point mass at a
coordinate where the reference law has full support.

\begin{lemma}[The signed repair--energy comparison]
\label{prop:signed-repair-Hellinger}
For $a,w,\delta\ge0$, put
\[
 h_\delta(a,w)=
   \bigl(\sqrt{w+\delta}-\sqrt{a+\delta}\bigr)^2,\qquad
 e=(w-a)_+.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 0\le h_\delta(a,w)&\le |w-a|,\\
 e&\le2h_\delta(a,w)
           +(a+\delta)\boldsymbol 1_{\{w>a\}},\\
 |w-a|&=2e+a-w.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:signed-repair-Hellinger-scalar}
\end{equation}
In particular, for any finite index set $S$, nonnegative
$a_p,w_p,\delta_p$ on $S$, and
$B=\{p\in S:w_p>a_p\}$, define
\[
 E=\sum_{p\in S}(w_p-a_p)_+,\quad
 H=\sum_{p\in S}h_{\delta_p}(a_p,w_p),\quad
 C=\sum_{p\in S}a_p,\quad W=\sum_{p\in S}w_p.
\]
The complete finite comparison is
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 H&\le2E+C-W,\\
 E&\le2H+\sum_{p\in B}(a_p+\delta_p)\\
  &\le2H+c\,\#B
       \quad\text{if }a_p+\delta_p\le c\ (p\in S).
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:signed-repair-Hellinger-sum}
\end{equation}
The signed mass correction $C-W$ has not been discarded.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $x=\sqrt{w+\delta}$ and $y=\sqrt{a+\delta}$.
Then $x,y\ge0$ and $x^2-y^2=w-a$.
Since $x+y\ge|x-y|$, their squared difference is at most
$|w-a|$. If $w>a$, the exact square identity
\[
 2(x-y)^2+y^2-(x^2-y^2)=(x-2y)^2
\]
gives the second inequality. If $w\le a$, its left side is
zero and the energy is nonnegative. Splitting into the same
two cases proves $|w-a|=2(w-a)_++a-w$.
Sum these statements on the specified finite sets.
The final comparison uses only the capacity bound on $B$.
\end{proof}

The maintained module \texttt{RepairHellinger.lean} proves
these scalar and finite-sum statements, including
common-floor positivity and the explicit repair-cardinality
consumer, in ten standard-axiom-audited declarations.
The infinite probability products and analytic consequences
in the following theorem are ordinary mathematics.

\begin{theorem}[A computable prime-Poisson overlap criterion for RH]
\label{thm:prime-Poisson-repair-RH}
Fix the finite character portfolio $\mathcal Q$, an integer
$k\ge2$, and either computable rational construction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:computable-rational-repair-detector} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:rational-LP-prime-repairs}.
Write $w_{p,k}$ for its output, $A(p)$ for the portfolio
capacity, and
\[
 \delta_p=p^{-4},\quad
 h_{p,k}=h_{\delta_p}(A(p),w_{p,k}),\quad
 H_{j,k}=\sum_{\substack{u\le p<2u\\p\ {\rm prime}}}h_{p,k},
 \qquad u=2^j.
\]
The repair support and repair mass below always refer to
the original weights $w_{p,k}$, before adding $\delta_p$.

For each fixed real $\sigma>0$, define independent Poisson
coordinates $(p,m)$, where $p$ is prime and $m\ge1$, with means
\begin{equation}
 \lambda^0_{p,m}(\sigma)
     =(A(p)+\delta_p)\frac{p^{-m\sigma}}m,\qquad
 \lambda^k_{p,m}(\sigma)
     =(w_{p,k}+\delta_p)\frac{p^{-m\sigma}}m.
 \label{eq:prime-Poisson-repair-means}
\end{equation}
Let $\mathsf P^0_{\sigma,\mathcal Q}$ and
$\mathsf P^k_{\sigma,\mathcal Q}$ be their product probability
measures on $\mathbb N_0^{\{(p,m)\}}$ with its product
sigma algebra. Every coordinate mean is strictly positive.

Put $f_\sigma(p)=-\log(1-p^{-\sigma})$.
For each integer cutoff $X\ge2$, the limiting affinity
of the complete prime-power laws at
primes $p\le X$ is
\begin{equation}
 \rho_{X,k}(\sigma)=
  \exp\left(-\frac12
       \sum_{\substack{p\le X\\p\ {\rm prime}}}
               h_{p,k}f_\sigma(p)\right).
 \label{eq:prime-Poisson-repair-affinity}
\end{equation}
Its decreasing limit $\rho_{\infty,k}(\sigma)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 \rho_{\infty,k}(\sigma)>0
 &\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \sum_p h_{p,k}p^{-\sigma}<\infty\\
 &\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \mathsf P^k_{\sigma,\mathcal Q}
           \text{ and }\mathsf P^0_{\sigma,\mathcal Q}
             \text{ are mutually absolutely continuous}.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:prime-Poisson-repair-equivalence}
\end{equation}
If the affinity is zero, the two product measures are
mutually singular.

Unconditionally, a reference zero with real part
$\beta>1/2$ has the following response: for every fixed $k$,
$1/2<\sigma<\beta$, and $0<\epsilon<\beta-\sigma$, infinitely
many dyadic $u=2^j$ satisfy
\begin{equation}
 \rho_{2u,k}(\sigma)
       \le\exp\left(-2^{-\sigma-3}
                         u^{\beta-\sigma-\epsilon}\right).
 \label{eq:prime-Poisson-bad-zero-response}
\end{equation}
Thus the product laws are mutually singular at every such
$\sigma$. No RH assumption is used for this response.

For $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$ and any prescribed unbounded set
$\mathcal K$ of integers $k\ge2$,
\begin{equation}
 \begin{aligned}
 {\rm RH}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad&
 \text{for every }\sigma\in\mathbb Q\cap(1/2,1)
 \text{ there exists a fixed }k\in\mathcal K\\[-2pt]
 &\text{with }\mathsf P^k_{\sigma,\{1\}}
          \text{ mutually absolutely continuous with }
                         \mathsf P^0_{\sigma,\{1\}}.
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:prime-Poisson-RH-criterion}
\end{equation}
In the RH direction, all sufficiently large $k\in\mathcal K$
work for a given $\sigma$. All finite affinities in
\eqref{eq:prime-Poisson-repair-affinity} are computable for
rational $\sigma>0$. This assertion does not decide positivity
of their infinite limit.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For positive means $\lambda,\mu$, the one-coordinate affinity
is obtained directly from the exponential series:
\[
 \sum_{n=0}^\infty
 \sqrt{e^{-\lambda}\frac{\lambda^n}{n!}
       e^{-\mu}\frac{\mu^n}{n!}}
   =e^{-(\lambda+\mu)/2}e^{\sqrt{\lambda\mu}}
   =e^{-(\sqrt\lambda-\sqrt\mu)^2/2}.
\]
Independence gives the product formula for finitely many
$(p,m)$. At a fixed prime, summing every $m\ge1$ gives
\[
 \sum_{m\ge1}\frac{p^{-m\sigma}}m
       =-\log(1-p^{-\sigma})=f_\sigma(p).
\]
The series is finite for $\sigma>0$; passing to its increasing
partial sums proves \eqref{eq:prime-Poisson-repair-affinity}.
No prime-power correction has been omitted.

The nonnegative exponent increases with $X$. Moreover
\begin{equation}
 p^{-\sigma}\le f_\sigma(p)
       \le\frac{p^{-\sigma}}{1-2^{-\sigma}}
                    \qquad(p\ {\rm prime},\ \sigma>0).
 \label{eq:prime-Poisson-primepower-comparison}
\end{equation}
The lower estimate is the $m=1$ term, and the upper estimate
bounds $1/m$ by $1$ in the geometric series.
This proves the first equivalence in
\eqref{eq:prime-Poisson-repair-equivalence}.
All coordinate Poisson laws assign positive probability
to every nonnegative integer. They are therefore mutually
absolutely continuous coordinate by coordinate.
Kakutani's product-measure dichotomy, in the form proved
by Kud\=o~\cite{KudoKakutani}, now gives the second equivalence
and the mutually singular alternative. Its local equivalence
hypothesis is why the same strictly positive floor was added
to both means, including at primes with $A(p)=0$.

We next retain the zero signal. Put
$c_{\mathcal Q}=1+\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}\varphi(q)$, so that
$A(p)+\delta_p\le c_{\mathcal Q}$ for every prime.
On the dyadic block the finite comparison gives
\begin{equation}
 \widehat E_{j,k}
   \le2H_{j,k}
       +c_{\mathcal Q}
            \#(\widehat{\mathcal B}_k\cap[u,2u))
   \le2H_{j,k}
       +2k c_{\mathcal Q}u^{1/(2k)}.
 \label{eq:prime-Poisson-retained-repair-signal}
\end{equation}
The rational repair detector already proves, for a reference
zero at real part $\beta>1/2$, that
$\limsup_j\log(1+\widehat E_{j,k})/\log u\ge\beta$.
Consequently $\widehat E_{j,k}\ge u^{\beta-\epsilon}$
at infinitely many sufficiently large dyadic scales.
One can use the limsup with $\epsilon/2$ first to absorb
the added $1$ without changing this conclusion.
Since $\beta-\epsilon>\sigma>1/2$ and $1/(2k)\le1/4$,
the last support term in
\eqref{eq:prime-Poisson-retained-repair-signal} is at most
$\widehat E_{j,k}/2$ on those scales. Thus
$H_{j,k}\ge u^{\beta-\epsilon}/4$.
For $p\in[u,2u)$,
$f_\sigma(p)\ge(2u)^{-\sigma}$; keeping just this block
in the nonnegative affinity exponent gives
\eqref{eq:prime-Poisson-bad-zero-response}.
It forces $\rho_{\infty,k}(\sigma)=0$.

For the RH direction specialize to $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$,
so $A(p)=1$. Write
$C_j=\#\{p\text{ prime}:u\le p<2u\}$ and
$W_{j,k}=\sum_{u\le p<2u}w_{p,k}$.
All sufficiently late cells are good, and their zeroth
moment errors give
\[
 |W_{j,k}-\nu([u,2u))|
          \ll_k u^{-3+1/(2k)}.
\]
Under RH the previously used prime-counting estimate and
the unconditional comparison of $\nu$ with the logarithmic
integral give
\[
 |C_j-W_{j,k}|\ll_k u^{1/2}\log u.
\]
For every fixed $\epsilon>0$, the existing rational repair
bound and the full signed energy comparison therefore imply
\begin{equation}
 0\le H_{j,k}
       \le2\widehat E_{j,k}+C_j-W_{j,k}
       \ll_{k,\epsilon}u^{1/2+1/(2k)+\epsilon}.
 \label{eq:prime-Poisson-RH-energy-bound}
\end{equation}
Fix $\sigma>1/2$. Choose $k\in\mathcal K$ large enough
that $1/(2k)<\sigma-1/2$, and then choose
$0<\epsilon<\sigma-1/2-1/(2k)$. The finitely many initial
blocks have finite energy, and the remaining series obeys
\[
 \sum_p h_{p,k}p^{-\sigma}
       \le\text{a finite initial contribution}
          +\sum_{j\ {\rm sufficiently\ large}}u^{-\sigma}H_{j,k}
       <\infty,
\]
by geometric summation. This proves the asserted probability
equivalence under RH, for every sufficiently large fixed $k$.

Conversely, if RH is false, reflection of the nontrivial
zeros supplies a zero with real part $\beta>1/2$.
Choose rational $\sigma$ with $1/2<\sigma<\beta$.
The unconditional bad-zero response makes the two product
laws mutually singular for every fixed $k$, contradicting
the right side of \eqref{eq:prime-Poisson-RH-criterion}.
This proves the reverse implication.
Finally, the rational weights and common floors are computable,
and at rational $\sigma$ the finite expression uses only
algebraic powers, square roots, logarithms and an exponential
of computable real numbers. This proves the finite
computability assertion.
\end{proof}

The new criterion interprets a hypothetical bad zero as
asymptotic statistical separation of explicit probability
laws. The sparse repair set retains the signal through
\eqref{eq:prime-Poisson-retained-repair-signal}; a positive
density of repair primes is unnecessary. An independent
upper bound on this energy or on the original repair mass
would still be needed to exclude the zero. The theorem
does not supply such an unconditional bound or change
the certified $7/8$ boundary. Kakutani's dichotomy and the
one-coordinate Poisson calculation are classical inputs;
the construction used here is the computable actual-prime
repair model with its quantitative capacity and zero signal.

\subsection{A computable deleted-prime class retaining the RH signal}
\label{sec:computed-deleted-prime-RH}

The weighted construction admits a criterion involving the
cardinality of a fixed, computable class of actual primes.
Two facts are needed. The positive-series continuation principle
can be applied to the opposite one-sided Euler quotient, provided
the reference has no real zero on the ray of continuation.
Also, an optimal LP vertex has few coordinates between its
zero and full-capacity states, including the constant-dual cases.

\begin{theorem}[Underweight transfer along the real axis]
\label{thm:underweight-real-axis-transfer}
Fix a nonempty finite character portfolio and
$1/2\le\tau\le\eta<1$. Let $w_p\ge0$, with
$\sum_p w_p p^{-\sigma}<\infty$ for every $\sigma>1$.
Assume its full Euler product $F_w$ has a nowhere-zero
holomorphic continuation on
$\{\Re s>\tau\}\setminus\{1\}$ and a pole of order $R$ at $1$.
Set
\[
 d_p=(A(p)-w_p)_+,\quad e_p=(w_p-A(p))_+,\quad
 D_w(X)=\sum_{\substack{p\le X\\p\ {\rm prime}}}d_p.
\]
Suppose
\[
 \mathcal Z_{\mathcal Q}(\sigma)\ne0\quad(\eta<\sigma<1),
 \qquad
 D_w(X)\ll_\epsilon X^{\eta+\epsilon}
                       \quad\text{for every }\epsilon>0.
\]
Then $\mathcal Z_{\mathcal Q}$ has no zeros on $\Re s>\eta$.

For the zeta reference $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$, the real-axis
nonvanishing condition is unconditional. In particular any
particular independently favorable $F_w$ as above has this
unconditional response to a zeta zero with real part $\beta>\tau$:
\begin{equation}
 \limsup_{j\to\infty}
 \frac{\log(1+D_{j,w})}{j\log2}\ge\beta,\qquad
 D_{j,w}=\sum_{\substack{2^j\le p<2^{j+1}\\p\ {\rm prime}}}
                          (1-w_p)_+.
 \label{eq:underweight-zero-readout}
\end{equation}
For general Dirichlet portfolios the displayed real-axis
hypothesis is retained; a possible exceptional real zero
cannot be discarded.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Define the complete products
\[
 E_-(s)=\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-d_p},\qquad
 E_+(s)=\prod_p(1-p^{-s})^{-e_p}.
\]
The assumed growth bound and dyadic summation make $E_-$
an absolutely normally convergent holomorphic unit on
$\Re s>\eta$. This includes every prime-power term in its
logarithm. The exact exponent identity
$w_p+d_p-A(p)=e_p$ gives, initially on $\Re s>1$,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{F_w(s)E_-(s)U_{\mathcal Q}(s)}
             {\mathcal Z_{\mathcal Q}(s)}
                 =E_+(s).
 \label{eq:underweight-full-positive-quotient}
\end{equation}
The nonsplit Euler factor $U_{\mathcal Q}$ is present.
The right side has nonnegative real Dirichlet coefficients
$a_n$, with $a_1=1$ and $a_p=e_p$ for each prime $p$.
Indeed the coefficient of $z^m$ in $(1-z)^{-e_p}$ is
$e_p(e_p+1)\cdots(e_p+m-1)/m!\ge0$; finite products and
monotone summation give the stated Dirichlet expansion.
It converges absolutely for $\Re s>1$ because $e_p\le w_p$.

Treat the left side of
\eqref{eq:underweight-full-positive-quotient} as a meromorphic
quotient, without assigning a value at a reference zero.
At every real $\sigma>\eta$, $\sigma\ne1$, it is holomorphic
in a neighborhood of $\sigma$: there are no reference
zeros on $(\eta,1)$ by hypothesis, and none on $(1,\infty)$
by the initial Euler product. At $1$, the two poles of
order $R$ cancel and give a removable, nonzero quotient.
The factors $E_-$ and $U_{\mathcal Q}$ are holomorphic
units there. Thus the real part of this quotient, with
its removable value at $1$, is a real analytic function
on the entire ray $\sigma>\eta$.
For real $\sigma>1$ it equals
$\sum_{n\ge1}a_n n^{-\sigma}$.

Choose an absolutely summable real anchor $c>1$.
The positive Dirichlet continuation principle proved in
\texttt{PositiveDirichletContinuation.lean} applies to
these coefficients and this real analytic function.
It gives actual summability of
$\sum_{n\ge1}a_n n^{-\sigma}$ for every $\sigma>\eta$.
The prime subseries therefore gives
\[
 \sum_p e_p p^{-\sigma}
   =\sum_{p\ {\rm prime}}a_p p^{-\sigma}
   \le\sum_{n\ge1}a_n n^{-\sigma}<\infty .
\]
Consequently the full logarithm of $E_+$ converges normally
on $\Re s>\eta$, since at a real $\sigma>\eta$
\[
 \sum_p\sum_{m\ge1}\frac{e_p p^{-m\sigma}}m
       \le\frac1{1-2^{-\sigma}}\sum_p e_p p^{-\sigma}<\infty.
\]
It defines a holomorphic unit there.

Cross multiplication now gives the meromorphic identity
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal Z_{\mathcal Q}(s)E_+(s)
           =F_w(s)E_-(s)U_{\mathcal Q}(s)
                         \qquad(\Re s>\eta).
 \label{eq:underweight-complete-cross-identity}
\end{equation}
It holds first on $\Re s>1$ and then throughout the connected
half-plane by meromorphic uniqueness. The right side is
nonzero away from the pole at $1$, and $E_+$ is a unit.
This excludes every reference zero on $\Re s>\eta$.

For $0<\sigma<1$, the alternating Dirichlet series gives
\[
 \zeta(\sigma)=
 \frac{\sum_{m\ge1}\bigl((2m-1)^{-\sigma}-(2m)^{-\sigma}\bigr)}
                 {1-2^{1-\sigma}}<0 .
\]
The paired numerator converges and is strictly positive.
Thus the real-axis hypothesis for zeta does not assume RH.
Finally, if the limsup in
\eqref{eq:underweight-zero-readout} were smaller than a zero
real part $\beta>\tau$, choose
$\max(\tau,\limsup)<\eta<\beta$.
Dyadic summation would give the required underweight bound
above $\eta$, and the result just proved would exclude that
zero. This contradiction proves the readout.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Few nonbinary coordinates at an optimal rational vertex]
\label{lem:rational-LP-binary-vertex}
On any good cell of the nonempty-portfolio rational program
\eqref{eq:rational-repair-linear-program}, every optimal
vertex $(v,e)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \#\{p\in S:v_p\notin\{0,A(p)\}\}\le k.
 \label{eq:rational-LP-vertex-sparsity}
\end{equation}
This includes all constant-dual and mass-deficit cases.
Choosing a deterministic minimizing rational vertex and
rounding its $v_p$ down to multiples of $M^{-1}$, without
the previous conic compression, gives weights $w^{\rm v}_{p,k}$
with
\begin{equation}
 \begin{gathered}
 \#\{p\in S:w^{\rm v}_{p,k}\notin\{0,A(p)\}\}\le k,\\
 \left|\sum_{p\in S}w^{\rm v}_{p,k}y_p^r-m_r\right|
                               \le3\tau\quad(0\le r<k),\\
 \sum_{p\in S}(w^{\rm v}_{p,k}-A(p))_+
           \le E_I^{\rm LP}\le E_{I,k}.
 \end{gathered}
 \label{eq:rational-LP-rounded-binary}
\end{equation}
Here $m_r$ are the true normalized model moments,
$\tau=u^{-3}$ and $M=u^4$ as before.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
At an optimum $e_p=(v_p-A(p))_+$, since otherwise decreasing
that $e_p$ alone would lower the objective while remaining
feasible. The zeroth moment tolerance gives
\[
 \sum_{p\in S}v_p\le\widetilde m_0+\tau\le H+2\tau<2Ru,
 \qquad H=R\nu(I).
\]
For the last strict inequality use $u\ge8$, $R\ge1$,
$H\le Ru/\log8<Ru$ and $2\tau<Ru$.
Thus every upper coordinate box for $v$ is strict.
Every upper box for $e$ is strict as well, since
$0\le e_p\le v_p$.

Suppose $T=\{p\in S:v_p\notin\{0,A(p)\}\}$ has more than
$k$ elements. Its rational moment columns
$(1,y_p,\ldots,y_p^{k-1})$ are linearly dependent over
$\mathbb Q$. Choose rational $c_p$, not all zero, supported
on $T$, with
\[
 \sum_{p\in S}c_p y_p^r=0\quad(0\le r<k).
\]
For a sufficiently small rational $t>0$, perturb in both signs:
\[
 v_p^\pm=v_p\pm tc_p,\qquad
 e_p^\pm=e_p\pm tc_p\boldsymbol1_{\{v_p>A(p)\}}.
\]
If $0<v_p<A(p)$, keep $e_p=0$ and choose $t$ small enough
to remain in that strict interval. If $v_p>A(p)$, keep
$e_p^\pm=v_p^\pm-A(p)>0$. Coordinates with $v_p=0$ or
$v_p=A(p)$ are unchanged. There are finitely many strict
margins, including the upper boxes, so one positive rational
$t$ preserves all of them in both directions.
All normalized moments are unchanged, hence all their
two-sided tolerance constraints are preserved.
The two feasible points are distinct and have $(v,e)$ as
their midpoint, contradicting the vertex property.
This proves \eqref{eq:rational-LP-vertex-sparsity}.

The earlier finite rational active-basis construction supplies
an optimal rational vertex. Fix an ordering of its finite
candidate bases and choose the first minimizing feasible
vertex; all comparisons are rational and decidable.
For $w^{\rm v}_{p,k}=\lfloor Mv_p\rfloor/M$, the coordinates
$0$ and $A(p)$ are unchanged, because $A(p)$ and $M$ are
integers. Rounding therefore creates no nonbinary coordinate.
It decreases each coordinate by less than $M^{-1}$, so each
normalized moment changes by at most $|S|/M\le u/M=\tau$.
The LP tolerance and target approximation contribute the
other two copies of $\tau$.
The positive overweight cannot increase.
Finally an exact optimal real quadrature is feasible for
the rational LP, since its exact moments differ from the
rational targets by at most $\tau$. Hence
$E_I^{\rm LP}\le E_{I,k}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The exact deleted-count ledger]
\label{lem:deleted-count-deficit-ledger}
Let $S$ be a finite set and $w_p\ge0$ on $S$. Define
\[
 Z=\{p\in S:w_p=0\},\quad T=\{p\in S:0<w_p<1\},\quad
 D=\sum_{p\in S}(1-w_p)_+.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}
 D=\#Z+\sum_{p\in T}(1-w_p),\qquad
                  0\le D-\#Z\le\#T .
 \label{eq:deleted-count-deficit-ledger}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
A zero coordinate contributes exactly $1$. A coordinate
in $T$ contributes exactly $1-w_p\in(0,1)$. Every remaining
coordinate has $w_p\ge1$ and contributes zero.
Partition the specified finite support into these three cases.
The maintained module \texttt{PartialCapacityCounts.lean}
proves this identity and its two count inequalities, including
their difference form, in seven standard-axiom-audited
declarations. The real-axis Euler transfer and the LP vertex
geometry remain ordinary proofs.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A predefined deleted-prime RH criterion]
\label{thm:computable-deleted-prime-RH}
For each fixed integer $k\ge2$, specialize the rational model
to $\mathcal Q=\{1\}$ and use the deterministic vertex and
rounding procedure of
Lemma~\ref{lem:rational-LP-binary-vertex}.
Use zero weights at primes below $8$ and on bad cells,
as in the previous construction. Define actual prime classes
\[
 \mathcal D_k=\{p\text{ prime}:w^{\rm v}_{p,k}=0\},\qquad
 \mathcal T_k=\{p\text{ prime}:0<w^{\rm v}_{p,k}<1\}.
\]
Membership in both classes is decidable by a terminating
finite computation from the integer inputs $p,k$.
Their definition uses no RH or zero-location assumption.

The full product $F_k^{\rm v}$ is independently favorable:
it has the same unconditional nowhere-zero continuation
on $\Re s>1/2$ and simple pole at $1$ as before.
For $j\ge3$, write $u=2^j$,
$S_j=\{p\text{ prime}:u\le p<2u\}$ and
\[
 N_{j,k}=\#(\mathcal D_k\cap S_j),\qquad
 D_{j,k}=\sum_{p\in S_j}(1-w^{\rm v}_{p,k})_+.
\]
At every such dyadic block,
\begin{equation}
 \#(\mathcal T_k\cap S_j)\le2k u^{1/(2k)},\qquad
             0\le D_{j,k}-N_{j,k}\le2k u^{1/(2k)}.
 \label{eq:computed-deleted-prime-counts}
\end{equation}
Every hypothetical zeta zero with real part $\beta>1/2$
forces, for every fixed $k$,
\begin{equation}
 \limsup_{j\to\infty}
       \frac{\log(1+N_{j,k})}{j\log2}\ge\beta.
 \label{eq:deleted-prime-count-zero-readout}
\end{equation}
In particular for every $\epsilon>0$ there are infinitely
many $j$ with $N_{j,k}\ge u^{\beta-\epsilon}$.

For every prescribed unbounded set $\mathcal K$ of integers
$k\ge2$, the exact equivalence is
\begin{equation}
 {\rm RH}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 N_{j,k}\ll_{k,\epsilon}u^{1/2+1/(2k)+\epsilon}
       \quad\text{for every }k\in\mathcal K,\ \epsilon>0.
 \label{eq:deleted-prime-RH-count-equivalence}
\end{equation}
There is also a one-model sufficient condition.
For any fixed $k\ge2$ and $1/2\le\eta<1$,
\begin{equation}
 \left[
   \forall\epsilon>0,\quad N_{j,k}\ll_\epsilon u^{\eta+\epsilon}
 \right]
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
       \zeta(s)\ne0\quad(\Re s>\eta).
 \label{eq:deleted-prime-fixed-k-zero-free}
\end{equation}
Thus finiteness of even one of these constructed deleted-prime
classes would imply RH.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The earlier good-cell test is a finite rational test after
enumerating the actual primes in the cell. The target moments
admit certified rational approximations.
The vertex is selected from finitely many rational active
bases, feasibility and objective comparisons are exact,
and rounding produces dyadic rational weights.
Given a prime $p\ge8$, its dyadic block and its unique
half-open cell are obtained by integer arithmetic.
Run this finite procedure on that cell and compare the
output at $p$ with $0$ and $1$. The finitely many smaller
primes have their specified zero weights.
This proves the claimed membership computation.

By Lemma~\ref{lem:rational-LP-binary-vertex}, there are at most
$k$ nonbinary coordinates on each good cell. On a bad cell
all coordinates are zero, so there are no partially weighted
coordinates. There are at most $2u^{1/(2k)}$ cells per block.
This proves the first inequality in
\eqref{eq:computed-deleted-prime-counts}.
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:deleted-count-deficit-ledger} on the
actual finite support $S_j$ for the second inequality.
